金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ AI 投資回報疑慮升溫，資金轉向債市避險：市場對大型科技企業 AI 基礎建設投資規模、融資
需求及未來獲利回收能力的疑慮升溫，導致科技股賣壓擴大，10 月 22 日 Nasdaq 指數下跌
約 2.6%。 隨著市場風險偏好下降 ， 避險資金轉向美國公債 ，10 年期公債殖利率單日下降約 19
個基點，創 2023 年 3 月以來最大單日跌幅，並於 23 日回升至 4.99%。市場關注焦點逐漸由
AI 投資規模轉向資本支出效益，後續大型科技企業財報及現金流表現，將成為評估 AI 產業評

價合理性的重要依據。
◼ 中東衝突推升通膨風險：中東衝突持續升溫，伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地阿拉伯，引
發市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂，推升布蘭特原油價格於10 月 23 日升至每桶 116.72 美元，單
週上漲約 6.2%，創 2022 年以來最高週收盤水準。能源價格攀升加劇全球通膨壓力，並壓抑
市場風險偏好。
◼ 法國政治動盪加劇財政疑慮，歐洲主權債風險重新受到關注：法國因生活成本上升爆發大規模
抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升
約 10 個基點至 4.88%附近，與德國公債利差擴大至約 144 個基點，創 2012 年以來最高收盤
水準 ， 法股單日下跌約1.5%， 歐元同步走弱 。 市場擔憂政治不確定性可能削弱財政改革進度 ，
並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線宜審慎，中期聚焦基本面穩健的 AI 企業：近期 AI 資本支出、融資需求及評價調整

使科技股波動加劇 ， 但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性 ，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變 。 隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升 ， 具技術優勢 、 財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利 ， 而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待 。
◼ 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：市場風險偏好降溫時，美國公債仍具一定
避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

市場焦點
美國
• 9月ISM服務業PMI降至54.9，雖商業活動與新訂單放緩 ，但就業回升至 50.1、訂單積壓增加 ，
顯示服務需求仍具韌性；另一方面，價格指數升至74.0，創2022年7月以來新高，供應商交貨
速度亦放緩 ，反映燃料 、原物料及供應鏈成本升高 ，景氣未明顯轉弱之際 ，通膨壓力反而重
新升溫；同時全球長債殖利率同步走高 ，AI基建投資延續企業成長動能 ，也提高資本需求 。
整體而言 ，美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但通膨與長端利率壓力同步升高 ，使金融條件維持偏緊 。
中國
• 彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與 AI普及下，中國產業快速發展出現供給過剩 ，產業獲利
能力面臨挑戰，未來三年中國AI產業將可能處於虧損狀態，不過即使模型價格下降，AI使用量
增加將帶動Token需求大幅成長，其中具有企業客戶、專有資料及生態優勢的AI龍頭，將比單

純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
黃金
• 歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵 ，加上長期通膨風險升溫 ，宏觀不確定性讓實體黃金
市場逆勢吸金 。本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易 ，實體黃金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中
期的支撐。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以投資組
合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。

美國9月ISM服務需求略降、成本壓力升高，景氣韌性與通膨風險並存
• 服務業維持擴張，需求略降但就業回穩：9月ISM服務業指數降至54.9(前月55.4)，連續27個月位於擴張區間；商業活動
指數降至56.5、新訂單降至 59.8，顯示服務需求較前月放緩 ，但就業指數回升至 50.1、重返擴張區間 ，訂單積壓升至
56.6，顯示內需仍具韌性，服務業尚未出現明顯轉弱訊號。
• 成本與供應鏈壓力升高 ，通膨降溫面臨干擾： 價格指數升至 74.0，創2022年7月以來新高 ，且為7個月來第6次高於70；
供應商交貨指數升至53.2，顯示交貨速度放緩，燃料、柴油、鋼材、銅及記憶體等成本上升，加上供應受限，使服務業
投入成本壓力擴大，通膨降溫進程面臨干擾。
• 市場觀察：美國服務業為經濟成長的重要支撐，目前經濟呈現「需求仍強、通膨升溫」的拉鋸格局，雖服務業景氣支撐

美國企業獲利，但成本上升可能推升利率波動，投資上宜偏向美國短中天期投資級債，兼顧較高收益率與利率風險控管；
股票則可聚焦具定價能力、獲利與現金流穩健的優質企業。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6 資料來源：ISM Services PMI Report， 2026.10.6

全球利率同步走高、AI投資持續擴張，長端美債壓力仍高
• 全球長債殖利率同步走高，反映利率環境重新定價：美國財政部長貝森特指出，近期美債殖利率攀升與德國、日本等主
要債市同步上行，反映全球資本成本重新定價，而非市場對美國資產失去信心；加上目前尚未出現資金大規模撤離美債
並轉向其他主權債券的現象，因此債市波動較偏向全球利率環境調整，而非金融系統性風險。
• AI投資增加資金需求，支撐較高利率環境：貝森特亦駁斥AI泡沫論，認為微軟、Alphabet及Meta等大型科技企業仍以
實際獲利與現金流支撐持續擴大AI資本支出，不同於過去缺乏基本面的科技泡沫；龐大的資料中心 、電力與半導體投資
需求提高整體資金成本，也成為推升長天期利率的重要因素之一。
• 市場觀察：目前市場經濟近況屬於利率高檔、成長尚穩的情境中，因而投資策略建議配置大型科技股、AI基建供應鏈及

受惠利差環境的金融股具相對優勢；債券配置則以中短天期投資級債優先，避免長天期債券受利率上行壓力侵蝕。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6

師預期第三季標普500企業獲利年增約29.5%，加上企業獲利預期持續上修，強化投資人對高利率環境下企業基本面的信心。
投資策略上，相較於單筆投資，此時的高成長與高波動的環境，更適合分批布局的定期定額策略來參與市場。

獲利成長；中長期建議維持日股正向看法，聚焦金融、AI、機器人、自動化及電力設備等具結構性成長題材的企業。

中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境
• 中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境 ：彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與
AI普及下，中國AI產業快速發展 ，但也逐漸出現供給過剩 ，使產業獲
利能力面臨挑戰 。開源模式雖有助於降低技術門檻 、應用普及 ，但也
使技術優勢難以長期維持 ，加上中國市場長期習慣低價甚至免費的數
位服務，多數AI業者難以透過訂閱模式獲利，轉而依賴利潤較低的API
服務作為主要收入來源 。目前中國已備案大型語言模型 (LLM)數量達
988個，包括DeepSeek、Kimi及智譜AI等新創企業快速崛起，進一步
加劇市場競爭。此外API服務逐漸被視為基礎公用服務，即使AI Agent
帶動詞元(Token)需求快速成長，也未必能同步提升獲利，由於開源模
型可由不同雲端業者部署 ，市場進入門檻降低 ，業者更難建立長期競

爭優勢。
• 後市展望： 考量龐大資本支出與持續價格競爭 ，未來三年中國 AI產業
整體仍可能處於虧損狀態 ，市場將形成「美國閉源、高毛利」與「中
國開源 、低成本 」模式 ，即使模型價格下降 ，AI 使用量增加將帶動
Token需求大幅成長 ，進而推升資料中心 、雲端服務及 AI晶片需求 ，
從投資角度來看，基礎設施供應商(晶片、伺服器、資料中心、雲端)受
惠程度可能高於模型開發商；而具有企業客戶 、專有資料及生態優勢
的AI龍頭，將比單純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。

人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台，有望提振中國經濟
和市場信心
• 人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台 ，有望提振中國經濟和市場信心 ：日前中國推出購房貸款貼息政策及下調
PSL(抵押補充貸款)利率，代表穩增長政策進一步加碼 ，有助於提振市場信心與經濟預期 ，此次政策不僅支持房地產市場 ，也
將「六張網」建設、科技創新、民營及小微企業 ，納入重點扶持範圍 ，透過增加再貸款額度 ，與提高支持比例 ，帶動投資、
就業及消費回升。短期A股有望在政策推動下出現築底反彈，其中基建投資、製造業投資、房市銷售及居民消費有機會改善，
同時PSL(抵押補充貸款)降息有助於流動性環境邊際寬鬆 ，降低市場對經濟增長的疑慮 ，後市來看企業獲利與信用環境有望逐
步修復，進一步鞏固A股慢牛格局。投資建議上房貸貼息政策有利房地產、建材、家居等產業鏈，金融業受惠資金成本下降與

信貸回升，食品飲料、零售、家電及服務消費則可望受惠於居民消費意願提升。

中國、日本減持美國公債的背後邏輯與差異
• 橋水基金創始人達利歐警告 ，美國公債正面臨中日兩國減持的風險 ，數據顯示這項警告已成事實 。2026年前七個月，中國
美國公債持倉減少660億美元至6,180億美元；同期日本持倉亦減少820億美元至1.1兆美元，扣除價格變動的估值效應後，
兩國實質持倉均大幅下滑。然而，兩國削減美國公債的背後動機並不相同，這將導致未來的減持軌跡出現明顯分化。
• 中國戰略性長期去美元化：1.降低地緣政治風險與轉向資產多元化 ，隨著人民幣在國際貿易結算中使用增加 ，市場流動性提
升，中國對美元儲備依賴度顯著下降。2.美債財政可持續性隱憂，美國政府債務持續攀升，引發對美國長期財政健康疑慮。
3.根據彭博經濟研究模型預測，在漸進式減持情境下，中國的美元儲備(以美債與機構債為主)到2050年可能減少約50%。

• 中國搭配戰術性節奏調整 (短期緩和因素)：儘管長期趨勢向下 ，但目前中美利差顯著 (美中30年期公債利差超過 350個基點)，
在美債殖利率維持高位期間，過快減持意味著實現債券價格下跌的虧損，且可能加劇人民幣升值壓力。因此，中國短中期內
傾向採取「戰略性減持、戰術性放緩」的靈活步調。
美國公債持倉：中國 vs 日本
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，美國財政部
中國預計將減少美元儲備
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.8，中國央行、國際貨幣基金組織、彭博經濟研究。

日本受政策利率與本土資本回流驅動
• 與中國的結構性考量不同，日本的減持主要出自週期性因素與匯率干預需求。
• 日本央行貨幣政策轉鷹： 日本央行正加快升息腳步 。隨著日債殖利率上揚 (30年期日債殖利率升至約 4.2%)，美日利差持續
收窄，吸引日本國內機構資金回流本土主權債市場。
• 匯率干預政策：日本為防範日圓過度貶值，日本財務省傾向出售部分海外美債資產以籌集外匯干預資金。若日圓再次面臨壓
力，此類拋售干預可能持續。因此，隨著日本央行逐步走向政策正常化，日本對美債的持倉大概會延續下滑軌跡。
• 小結與投資建議：美國公債作為全球無風險資產的地位正面臨結構性挑戰，中國的「長期去美元化」與日本的「本土利率回
升資金回流」將形成雙重壓力，這意味著美債殖利率短期內難大幅下行，美國財政部發債成本居高不下，進而推高全球資本

的融資成本。投資債券配置建議採取「中短天期＋階梯式配置」策略，長天期美國公債(20年以上)受發債供給過剩及中日兩
國長期減持影響，價格波動較大。建議投資人適度降低長天期美國公債暴險，轉向防禦性較佳的中短天期美國公債或高品質
高評級公司債，以鎖定當前較高殖利率同時控管利率風險。
殖利率差：美元與人民幣 vs 美元與日圓
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.29

黃金市場資金流向分歧，實體投資有望支撐中長期金價表現
• 利率與匯率因素，壓抑金價近期表現：9月以來，美國公債殖利率飆升、美元指數走揚，由於黃金本身不孳息，資金傾向轉
往固定收益市場，疊加美元走強壓抑國際購買意願，金價自8月底高點至今已下修約11%。
• 9月資金流向分歧，實體投資強、期貨市場弱：根據世界黃金協會統計，9月在金價下跌之際，全球實體黃金ETF卻吸引大量
資金流入，流入約100億美元(67噸)。另一方面，COMEX黃金期貨市場投機部位大幅下降 ，避險基金與其他機構投資人的
多頭部位明顯減少，造成期貨市場賣壓增加，進一步推動金價下跌。
• 實體黃金市場逆勢吸金反應投資人的避險需求：歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵，加上美伊衝突未解及AI算力需求強
勁，長期通膨風險升溫，宏觀的不確定性讓實體黃金市場逆勢吸金。從數據上來看，本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易，實體黃

金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中期的支撐 。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以
投資組合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，2026.10.7。

資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。

7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

息日前一日之淨值×100%。

績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。

美國消費回暖、房市降溫，Fed 政策轉向與川習會成市場焦點 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額 7,739 億美元，月增 1.2%、年增 6.0%，
扭轉前月下滑走勢；核心零售(扣除汽車、零件及加油站)月增 1.2%。其
中非實體零售商、加油站及電子家電商店銷售成長，顯示美國消費需求
仍具支撐力道。 
⚫ 8 月美國住宅市場表現分化，建築許可年化總數降至 139.4 萬戶，月減
2.7%；新屋開工降至 127.5 萬戶，月減2.6%，惟單戶住宅開工升至 91.8
萬戶 、 創近5 個月新高 。 另一方面 ， 在建住宅年減3.2%、 完工年減27.1%，
顯示高利率與需求不確定性仍抑制整體住宅建設活動。 
⚫ 9 月聯邦公開市場委員會(FOMC)決議以 12 比 0 一致升息 1 碼至 3.75%
至 4.00%，為 2023 年 7 月以來首度升息。委員會認為美國經濟及勞動
市場仍具韌性，惟通膨持續高於 2%目標，因此採取升息行動並重申致
力維持價格穩定，政策立場轉趨鷹派；同時刪除前瞻指引措辭，強調未
來政策將依據經濟數據及展望調整。最新經濟預測(SEP)顯示，2026 年
GDP 成長率上修至 2.3%、失業率下修至 4.1%，PCE 及核心 PCE 通膨
率則分別上修至 3.7%及 3.4%，利率中位數升至 4.1%。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 在 9 月 24 日美國華府「川習會」前，美國財長貝森特與中國副總理何
立峰於紐約會談，聚焦AI、關稅、貿易休戰及關鍵礦產供應等議題。市
場預期雙方將以維持對話、避免貿易緊張升級為主，短期內難有重大突
破。 
⚫ 美國在 USMCA 談判中要求墨西哥提高 AI 伺服器及半導體產品的北美
原產地比重，並限制中國等非北美零組件使用，以防堵企業藉由墨西哥
組裝規避美國關稅。由於墨西哥已成為美國 AI 伺服器重要供應基地，
若新規定上路 ， 恐促使包括台廠在內的供應鏈提高北美在地採購與生產
比重，進一步重塑北美 AI 硬體供應鏈布局。 
⚫ 美國總統川普簽署《2026 年 Lindsey O. Graham 制裁俄羅斯與伊朗法
案》 ，強化對俄、伊制裁，並授權對大量採購俄羅斯能源國家商品課徵
最高 100%關稅。

屋貸款最高成數由 6 成提高至 7 成 ， 以及刪除購地貸款須於一定期間內
動工興建之規定。 
短評 ⚫ 本次理監事會議的政策方向呈現利率維持審慎偏緊基調 、 房市管制微幅鬆綁。

一、 美國—消費回暖、房市降溫，Fed 政策轉向與川習會成市場焦點 
2026 年 8 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額經季節性調整後為 7,739 億美元，較
上月增加 1.2%，優於 7 月修正後月減 0.5%的表現，年增率亦達 6.0%，顯示美國
消費支出動能於 8 月明顯回升。若扣除汽車及零件銷售，零售額月增1.4%、年增
6.9%；進一步扣除汽車、零件及加油站後，核心零售銷售月增1.2%、年增 5.6%，
反映核心消費需求維持穩健成長。從主要業別表現觀察，非實體零售商銷售額月
增 2.6%、加油站月增 3.1%、電子及家電商店月增 1.6%，食品服務及飲酒場所亦
月增 1.2%，顯示線上消費、能源相關支出及服務性消費持續成長；另一方面，建
築材料與園藝用品經銷商月減 0.2%，對整體零售表現形成部分抵銷。整體而言，
8 月美國零售銷售由前月下滑轉為明顯成長，且扣除汽車及能源相關項目後仍維
持穩健增幅，顯示美國消費需求仍具支撐力道，對經濟活動形成正面挹注，惟後
續仍須持續關注物價變動、金融情勢及消費信心對零售支出的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國住宅市場持續呈現分化走勢，建築許可年化總數降至 139.4
萬戶，月減 2.7%，惟較去年同期增加 3.5%；其中單戶住宅建築許可 87.8 萬戶，
月減 1.8%，5 戶以上多戶住宅建築許可 46.7 萬戶，月減 3.1%，顯示住宅開發規
劃動能較前月略有降溫 。 另一方面 ， 新屋開工年化數降至127.5 萬戶 ， 月減2.6%、
年減 1.2%，其中單戶住宅開工回升至 91.8 萬戶，月增 7.6%、年增 5.2%，為近 5
個月最高水準 ； 惟5 戶以上多戶住宅開工降至 34.4 萬戶 ， 月減22.5%、 年減15.5%，
拖累整體開工表現。施工方面，在建住宅年化數為 127.1 萬戶，月增 0.3%，但較
去年同期減少 3.2%，顯示住宅建設規模仍呈收斂趨勢；完工年化數則降至 112.8
萬戶，月減 11.9%、年減 27.1%，反映住宅供給落成速度明顯放緩。整體而言，8
月美國住宅市場呈現單戶住宅需求相對具支撐、但多戶住宅及整體營建活動偏弱
的格局，顯示在高利率與需求前景仍具不確定性的環境下，建商投資態度仍趨審
慎，後續仍須關注利率走勢及房市需求變化對住宅市場的影響。

下：(一)儘管地緣政治風險推升經濟前景不確定性，美國經濟活動仍維持穩健擴
張，國內支出展現韌性，生產力成長與資本投資維持強勁，勞動市場表現穩定，
就業增幅大致與勞動力成長相符，失業率變動不大；(二)通膨仍高於聯準會 2%目
標，委員會認為近期數據尚未充分顯示通膨正以足夠速度回落至目標水準，因此
決議升息 1 碼，以支持通膨更及時回歸 2%目標，並重申將致力實現價格穩定；
(三)本次會議在政策溝通上出現調整，聲明刪除過去偏向寬鬆的前瞻指引措辭並
簡化內容；主席 Kevin Warsh 於記者會中明確表示不提供前瞻指引，亦不預判未
來會議結果 ， 強調後續政策行動仍將依據最新經濟數據及整體經濟展望決定 ；(四)
最新 SEP 顯示，2026 年實質 GDP 成長率預測中位數由 2.2%上修至 2.3%，失業
率由 4.3%下修至 4.1%；PCE 及核心 PCE 通膨率則分別上修至 3.7%及 3.4%，顯
示通膨壓力仍具黏性 。 利率方面 ，點陣圖顯示 2026 年底聯邦基金利率預測由 3.8%
升至 4.1%，2027 年底維持 4.1%，官員仍保留進一步升息可能，整體政策立場較
6 月轉趨鷹派；(五)主席 Kevin Warsh 表示，美國經濟與勞動市場維持韌性，通膨
仍高於目標且地緣政治風險升溫 ， 為本次升息主因 。 未來聯準會不提供前瞻指引 ，
也不預設利率路徑，將依經濟數據與展望機動調整政策。 
在 9 月 24 日美國華府白宮舉行之 「川習會」 前 ， 美國財長貝森特(Scott Bessent)
與中國國務院副總理何立峰於紐約摩根大通總部舉行會談，美國貿易代表葛里爾
(Jamieson Greer) 亦共同參與。雙方將就人工智慧 (AI)、關稅、貿易休戰及關鍵礦
產供應等議題交換意見，並為川普與習近平會晤預作準備。討論重點包括將於11
月 10 日到期之美中貿易休戰安排、中國稀土磁材及關鍵礦產出口供應情況，以
及 AI 模型安全與治理機制等議題。此外，雙方亦將檢視今年 5 月北京會談後尚
待落實之事項，包括降低部分非戰略性商品關稅、中國增加美國農產品採購及波
音客機採購計畫等。市場普遍預期本次會談將以維持既有合作架構及避免貿易緊
張升級為主要目標，短期內出現重大突破的可能性仍相對有限，美中關稅、關鍵
礦產供應及 AI 治理議題預料將成為本次川習會的重要焦點。 
美國政府近期在美墨加協定(USMCA)檢討談判中，要求墨西哥針對人工智慧
(AI)伺服器、半導體及相關硬體產品建立更嚴格的原產地規範，擬限制來自中國
等非北美地區零組件占比，並可能參照汽車產業的區域含量標準，以防堵外國企
業透過墨西哥組裝後出口美國，規避美國對中國商品實施的關稅與貿易限制。市
場分析指出，在美國 AI 基礎建設需求帶動下，墨西哥已成為重要 AI 伺服器生產

基地，今年上半年 AI 資料中心伺服器出口金額達 830 億美元，其中 94%銷往美
國，並超越汽車成為對美最大出口項目。由於鴻海等台灣電子代工廠在墨西哥設
有 AI 伺服器生產據點，若相關規範正式上路，供應鏈可能被迫提高北美在地採
購及生產比重，進一步重塑北美 AI 硬體供應鏈布局。美墨雙方預計 9 月底於華
府展開新一輪談判，惟相關原產地門檻、過渡期間及適用範圍尚未定案。 
美國總統川普已於 9 月 18 日簽署《2026 年 Lindsey O. Graham 制裁俄羅斯與
伊朗法案》，該法案此前分別以參議院 86 票對 11 票、眾議院 262 票對 159 票通
過。法案除擴大對俄羅斯政府官員、金融機構及協助能源運輸的「影子艦隊」制
裁外，亦授權總統對持續大量購買俄羅斯石油及天然氣的第三國商品課徵最高
100%關稅，其中中國、印度等俄羅斯能源主要買家可能受到影響。法案目的在於
削弱俄羅斯能源出口收入及其支撐烏俄戰爭的財政來源，並強化對伊朗的相關制
裁。 
二、歐元區—美歐矛盾與歐中貿易協商在即，增添工業復甦變數 
 歐元區 7 月工業生產較 6 月下滑 0.1%，為連續兩個月衰退，其中非耐久消費
財產出減少 1.6%，抵銷中間財、資本財、消費耐久財分別成長0.3%、0.5%、0.9%
的影響，主要是美伊衝突帶來的庫存儲備需求持續消退，抵銷德國軍備採購及消
暑用品消費增加的影響。以國別而言，德國工業生產下跌1.5%最為疲軟，且為該
國近一年最大跌幅，主要是部分汽車大廠決定提前進行歲修，故預期待歲修結束
後，8、9 月份可望出現汽車庫存回補的生產需求，此外，在德國軍備採購需求的
支持下 ， 德國7 月工業訂單仍大幅成長 2.5%， 相關產出可望在第三季末及第四季
陸續反映；法、義、西工業生產月增率則分別為-0.4%、0.7%、0.6%。 
 整體而言，美伊衝突爆發後，能源密集產業普遍增產，但該類產業從 6 月起
已有較明顯的趨緩跡象，其他主要產業則大致呈現橫向波動，顯示工業生產趨勢
仍屬溫和。惟近期歐盟提議讓加拿大成為歐盟準會員國，引發美國不滿，可能進
一步升高美歐貿易與外交摩擦；此外，歐盟與中國預定於 10 月 8 日針對貿易失
衡問題進行重要協商 ， 後續若涉及貿易措施調整 ， 亦可能影響歐元區製造業表現 。

7 月核心機械訂單月減 3.7%，其中製造業訂單小幅下滑，主要受到化學
產業訂單減少影響，惟電子產品及電氣機械訂單持續成長，反映人工智慧相

關產業及供應鏈企業仍積極擴充產能，支撐資本支出動能。 雖然非製造業訂
單則因資通訊及資訊服務業需求回落影響而表現較弱，但在人力短缺與數位
轉型需求支撐下，投資動能仍具韌性。綜合來看，企業獲利穩健及AI 投資熱
潮延續，預期日本企業資本支出仍將維持成長態勢。 
8 月出口年增 19.3%，半導體相關產品仍為主要成長動能，其中半導體等
電子零件出口大幅成長 52.3%。從地區來看，對美國、中國及歐盟出口分別
成長 24.9%、20.6%及 11.0%，半導體等電子零組件對中國出口金額，以及對
美國 、 歐盟的半導體製造設備出口均較去年同期倍增，顯示全球 AI 及高科技
產業投資需求持續支撐日本出口表現。 
8 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 1.9%，主要受到政府恢復電力與天然
氣補貼，以及米價回落抑制食品價格所致。從細項來看，石油產品價格上漲
帶動家庭消費品等核心商品價格續漲；服務類通膨除通訊費用趨穩外，其他
服務項目已開始出現漲價跡象。展望未來，能源成本上升及企業將 薪資成本
逐步轉嫁至售價，預料將推動通膨壓力由商品擴散至更多服務領域，使通膨
維持緩步升溫趨勢，並進一步支持央行持續推動貨幣政策正常化。 
日本央行於 9 月 17、18 日召開貨幣政策會議，宣布將政策利率調升 1 碼
至 1.25%。雖然政策聲明整體基調變化不大，但仍持續強調通膨上行風險，
並指出目前金融條件仍相對寬鬆。 值得關注的是 ， 本次決策出現兩張反對票 ，
顯示委員會內部對經濟與通膨前景看法分歧加劇，一派認為當前經濟與通膨
動能尚不足以支持升息，另一派則認為通膨升溫風險高於預期。在會後記者
會上，總裁植田和男指出，日本貨幣政策已進入新的階段，由過去較重視通
膨低於目標的風險，轉為兼顧通膨上、下行風險，並強調未來升息步調將視
經濟與物價發展而定，不會預先承諾特定路徑。此外，他也表示 主要國家央
行升息對日本匯率及金融環境具有重要影響，因此將持續納入政策考量。雖
然總裁未透露最終利率水準，但相關談話暗示目前 1.25%的政策利率可能尚
未達到中性水準，日本央行亦未排除未來進一步升息的可能性。整體而言，
日本央行對通膨前景看法趨於樂觀，政策立場仍偏向緊縮，預期若未來數季
通膨持續走揚，日本央行仍有望於 12 月再度升息。

四、中國—8 月內需持續疲乏，與歐美的關係將為近期關注重點 
在內需方面，1-8 月累計零售額年增率較 2025 年同期增加 1.1%，較 1-7 月累
計的 1.2%持續下降。其中 8 月零售額年增率放緩 0.2 個百分點至 0.4%，反映以
舊換新的補貼力道不如過去兩年水準，加上房市低迷，以及對景氣波動較為敏感
的消費耐久財需求進一步下滑 ，8 月家具 、 汽車 、 裝潢消費分別年減7.9%、18.5%、
11.8%。另外，1-8 月固定資產投資年減幅由 6.7%擴大至 7.2%，反映美伊衝突、
政府主導去化過剩產能、美國貿易戰等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎縮產
業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等。同期間房地產開發
投資額年減幅由 19.2%擴大至 19.9%、施工面積年減幅則擴大為 13.2%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動 ， 抵銷內需疲軟的影響 ，8 月工業生產年增率較 7
月回升 0.7 個百分點至 5.2%(1-8 月工業生產年增率持平 1-7 月水準為 5.3%)。依
產業別來看，8 月份高科技產品仍是主要成長動能，包含 IC(成長 20.6%)、工業
機器人(34.6%)、服務機器人(5%)、 新能源車(21.9%)等 ； 惟受到原油供應減少以及
能源價格上漲壓力影響，化纖、水泥、玻璃、鋼材等產量持續衰退，另因 3C 關
鍵物料漲價影響，手機產量衰退幅度高達 22.3%，太陽能電池則因取消出口退稅
政策跌幅高達 12.9%。展望未來，若9 月 24 日川習會有正向發展，美中關稅休戰
得以延長 ， 且歐中貿易摩擦未進一步惡化 ， 將有助於穩定中國出口與製造業景氣 ，
抵銷內需疲軟的經濟壓力。 
五、印度及東南亞—印度通膨持穩，AI 需求持續帶動新、馬出口表現 
印度:受美伊衝突延宕與不利的氣候條件影響，8 月批發物價指數(WPI)年增率
揚升至 9.92%，其中，燃料價格飆漲22.9%，食品價格漲幅達 5.7%，另受化學
品、菸草、塑橡膠、基本金屬漲幅均逾一成影響，製造業通膨加速上揚8.4%。
8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 4.82%，為 2024 年 12 月以來最高，食品
飲料（5.95%）、交通運輸（4.6%）餐飲和住宿服務（8.38%）及個人照護/社

五、台灣—央行維持利率不變並適度鬆綁不動產信用管制措施 
台灣央行於 9 月 17 日召開理監事會議，決議維持政策利率(重貼現率)於 2%
不變，央行指出，雖然國際油價維持高檔、服務類通膨仍具黏性，但國內通膨仍
屬可控範圍，2026 年 1 至 8 月通膨年增 1.84%，預估 2027 年可回落至 2%以下；
同時考量中東地緣政治、美國經貿政策及 AI 產業發展等不確定因素，因此採取
審慎觀望態度，暫不升息。 
此外，央行認為先前實施之選擇性信用管制已逐漸見效，房市交易降溫、不
動產貸款集中度下降，因此適度調整相關措施，包括將自然人第二戶購屋貸款最
高成數由 6 成提高至 7 成，並刪除購地貸款須切結於一定期間內動工興建之規
定，改由金融機構依授信實務審慎評估辦理。整體而言，本次政策方向呈現利率
維持審慎偏緊基調、房市管制微幅鬆綁。

CHPT
Valuation
We value CHPT using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum and the structural shift towards high-end
testing. Our target price of NT$4,350 is derived from applying 35x to 2027E EPS, implying the company's average forward PE
since 2024 +1 SD, which we believe is justified given the company's entrenched position in the 2nm/3nm testing segment,
rising contribution from US AI GPU & AI ASIC projects, and the unique competitive advantage of its "All-in-House" vertical

integration model. With these structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to value it as an AI/HPC play
(rather than a consumer electronics stock), we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant system rates the CHPT stock as High Risk for its share price volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price, and to our earnings forecasts, include: 1) margin

compression from lower-than-expected utilization rates; 2) execution delays in concentrated US AI GPU and ASIC programs;
3) intensifying competition in testing boards from global peers; and 4) structural gross margin dilution from a shift towards
high-end burn-in boards.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

작성자(한동희)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN DWELLING
APPROVALS BULLETIN
Units lift the headline
2 November 2026
Key points
• Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth
in September, although the annual pace
moved into contraction at –0.8%yr with
the detail suggesting the underlying
cycle is starting to turn down.

• The headline increase was driven by
another volatile rise in private unit
approvals (+16.6%) which more than
offset a significant decline in private
detached house approvals (–5.3%).
• Units drove gains in NSW and
Queensland. WA, SA and Victoria all
recorded declines. Detached dwelling
approvals fell across all states.
• The total value of residential approvals
fell 2.8%mth but a sharp rise in non-
residential approvals – led by the
data centres – saw the total value of

building approvals rise 13%mth.
• Interest rate increases, weakness in
established housing markets, rising costs
and pessimistic consumer sentiment
are likely to remain headwinds.
Total dwelling
approvals
+5.1%mth,
–0.8%yr

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth in September, while
the annual pace moved into contraction at –0.8%yr. The
result was well above both Westpac’s expectation for a
7.0%mth decline and the market expectation for a 2.0%mth
rise. Unit approvals continued to drive headline volatility,

surging in September, while detached dwellings, which
provide a better read on the underlying trend, recorded a
sizeable decline.
Note that most figures in the charts and table are presented on
a rolling-three month basis to smooth out monthly volatility.
Private detached dwelling approvals fell 5.3%mth in
September, unwinding August’s upwardly-revised 5.4%mth
rise (+1.7ppts). Recent resilience has kept the segment
elevated, with approvals remaining above 10,000 and the

annual pace at 9.6%yr. However, the quarterly profile provides
a clearer read on the underlying trend and suggests detached
dwelling approvals are beginning to respond to recent interest
rate increases (see here). Approvals rose just 0.9%qtr in
September vs 3.1%qtr in June and 5.9%qtr in March.
Private unit approvals remained volatile, rising 16.6%mth in
September following 21.7%mth decline in August. Strength
was led by high-rise approvals, while low-mid-rise approvals

recorded a more modest increase. Despite the monthly rise,
the annual pace fell further into contraction at –20.5%yr.
Among the states, NSW recorded the strongest result,
with private approvals rising 35.3%mth led by a surge in
private unit approvals. Queensland also recorded an increase
(+9.3%mth), while WA (–2.9%mth), SA (–3.5%mth) and
Victoria (–29.0%mth) all declined.
In value terms, residential building approvals fell 2.8%mth,
driven by a 3.3%mth decline in new residential approvals,

partially offset by a 0.8%mth rise in renovation activity.
Non-residential approvals once again reversed sharply, rising
45.9%mth, as the data centre-dominated ‘other commercial’
segment recorded an 8-fold increase on a non-seasonally
adjusted basis. The total value of building approvals rose
13.0%mth in September. On a non-seasonally adjusted basis,
the value of building approvals rose 8.3%mth overall, but
declined 3.0%mth when excluding the ‘other commercial’

segment.
September’s result reinforces the importance of looking
through headline volatility in unit approvals. While total
approvals rose in the month, detached dwelling approvals
slowed on a quarterly basis and fell across all states, pointing
to softer underlying momentum. The annual pace of total
approvals also moved into contraction for the first time
since January. Interest rate increases, the sharp downturn
in established housing markets and rising costs are likely to

weigh on further growth, with pessimistic consumer sentiment
an additional headwind (see here).
Detached approvals point to softer momentum

Document 320: Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-03)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-03).pdf

Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:16 October 30 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI. Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in
the month, largely owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in domestic and
international holiday travel & accommodation. The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than

anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).

### Page 2

While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation – housing in particular looks to be cooling faster
than expected as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are still showing persistence.
Highlighting this is market services ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator of price
pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail
points to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for another chunky read in Q4.

With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts, and some of the energy risks flagged
then materialising, we continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp rate hike to
4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part
of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding
instead to wait for further information before taking the next step with policy. The decision was made

4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A 25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening
in 2027 (Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems probable; however, each
decision will depend on fuel prices, inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October meeting. Based on prior comments by key
members, the decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed

President Hammack’s dissents in favour of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in
September). Chair Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment (evinced by Q3 GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains

elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual inflation
to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target. This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help
the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December.
Thereafter, the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through
2027; although, in the absence of another shock, we expect them to remain on hold.

The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view on the breadth of growth, the 3.4%
annualised gain for GDP benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household consumption,
12.7% growth in non-residential investment as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment was effectively offset by net exports’ drag
given most of the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is imported. This is not to say that

US firms won’t benefit from the investment once complete, but simply that much of the initial return
goes to the foreign businesses who provide the necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks remain for the consumer, the increase
in nominal spending seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis, consumption flat in the
month following a 0.5% gain in August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the outlook is

### Page 3

Related articles
expected to see household consumption growth average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr
medium-term target as the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline and core
measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy rates unchanged in October. Of note from the
meeting communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks for inflation, but also downside

risks for growth. "While the full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying inflation has
remained broadly stable so far and wages do not show a material response". "Financing conditions
have [also] tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth". In the press conference,
President Lagarde noted that, between the October and December meetings, a significant amount of
new economic information will be released, allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.

Based on the October meeting communications, we continue to expect a rate hike at the December
meeting but believe this will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the spectrum of risks
Europe faces is broad and skewed towards inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that she will step down as ECB President in
February ahead of the scheduled conclusion of her term next October.

Document 347: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged  
Proportion of US population by age range (%)

Source: Human Mortality Database; Bank of America Institute calculations

### Page 2

2 01 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Exhibit 3: Older people spend less time working and more time

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

nuanced picture. While households aged 61-75 do spend relatively less on restaurants and bars than the overall population, they 
appear to be narrowing this gap. But for those older than 75 this is not the case. In our view, the pandemic may still be weighing 
on dining-out spending among this older age group (Exhibit 6).

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Document 462: US Midterms_ The cost of living is now a pivotal issue (2026-11-02)

Report source: BNP Paribas

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026EDITORIAL
US MIDTERMS: THE COST OF LIVING IS NOW A PIVOTAL ISSUE
1 AP-NORC, October 2026.
2 Jared Bernstein, Daniel Posthumus, ‘The way we were: Price-level shocks and consumers’ memories’, SIEPR, May 2026.
3 72% of registered voters rank ending the war with Iran as one of the top priorities for the next Congress, including 60% of Republicans (AP-NORC, October 2026).

4 Oleg Korenok, David Munro, Jiayi Chen, ‘Inflation and attention thresholds’, GLO Discussion Paper Series, No. 1175, Global Labour Organization, 2022.
5 Rajashri Chakrabarti, Thu Pham, Beck Pierce, Maxim L. Pinkovskiy, ‘Tracking the K-shaped economy: who’s driving spending?’, Federal Reserve Bank of New York, Liberty
Street Economics, 1st May 2026.
6 The year 2020 is excluded due to the disruptions caused by lockdowns.
In the United States, growth is holding up, unemployment remains low and consumer spending shows no sign of slowing.

On paper, that should flatter the president’s party ahead of the midterms. Yet, Republicans could lose control of Congress.
The rise in the cost of living, which weighs on households far more than macroeconomic aggregates suggest, could be one
of the reasons. On top of persistent inflation, rekindled by the war in Iran, there is also a slowdown in wage growth and
rising housing costs. The election could hinge on affordability, yet whichever party wins will lack the means to address it.

A change in the majority in Congress will nonetheless have an impact on the country’s economy.
On 3 rd November, Americans will elect a new Congress for the next
two years. With Republicans currently in control of the White House,
the House of Representatives and the Senate, the balance of power in
Washington could shift. Polls suggest that the House, at least, could
swing to the Democrats. Beyond the new balance of power, the vote
will reflect how voters view the way the country is being run. Here, we

focus on the cost of living.
INFLATION: THE INCUMBENTS’ BURDEN
The incumbent party goes into the election with two handicaps.
The first is structural: no matter who holds the White House, the pre -
sident’s party almost always loses seats in midterm elections. The
second stems from the combination of robust but uneven growth
and persistent inflation, which is undermining household confidence.
Pre-election polls1 confirm this assessment: the economy and the cost-

of-living top voters’ worries.
Inflation has overshot the Fed’s target every year since 2021. Prices are
up 28% over five years: 31% for housing (not including mortgage rates,
which have more than doubled), and 29% for food. This shock is felt all
the more keenly as it is the most severe in forty years and follows on
from decades of low inflation2. Furthermore, it hits the lowest-income
households the hardest. In addition, the election comes after an ener-

gy-driven rise in inflation. Energy accounts for the entire gap between
the latest CPI reading (3.7% y/y in September) and the last figure re -
corded before the conflict in the Middle East started (2.4% y/y).
Fuel has made things worse. The rise in the price of a gallon (46%
between February and October) caused by the Iran war — which is
unpopular even amongst Republican voters3 — is an aggravating factor.
In 2026, inflation only slightly crossed the ‘attention threshold’, the

3.6%y/y rate beyond which households start to regard it as problema -
tic4. Fuel is a recurring, unavoidable expense whose price is directly
observable, unlike many items in the CPI basket. This results in a subs-
tantial gap between perceived inflation and measured inflation.
Inflation must be viewed alongside the other drivers of households’
well-being. Today, wages, payrolls and the housing market are moving
the wrong way for most Americans.
IT'S NOT JUST PRICES

The 2022 midterms suggest caution in reading the polls. Democrats
defied expectations of a ‘Republican wave’, retaining control of the Se-
nate and losing the House by just a few seats, even though inflation
averaged 8% that year. This reminds us that inflation must be viewed
alongside the other drivers of households’ well-being. Indeed, how
people experience the cost of living depends on the interplay between
prices, wages and housing. In 2022, wages, payrolls and the housing

market were all growing faster than in 2026. Today, all three are mo -
ving the wrong way for most Americans.
Wage growth is slowing just as inflation picks up again. In September
2026, average hourly earnings were up 3% y/y in nominal terms, the
slowest rate since 2018, and now below inflation. Real wage earnings
are therefore falling (-0.7% y/y in September, with six out of nine mon-
ths showing a decline in 2026). The rise in the cost of living is thus

compounded by a net loss of purchasing power. The Atlanta Fed reports
that wage growth is lower for the bottom quartile of earners (the 25%
of employees with the lowest pay) than for the top quartile (the 25%
with the highest pay): 3.8% y/y vs. 4.4% on average since late 2024,
following 10 years in which the gap favoured the bottom quartile. Top
earners also benefit most from the wealth effect linked to stock mar -
ket valuations. This explains the resilience of aggregate consumption.

However, this rests on a relatively narrow base, mostly households
earning more than USD 125,000/year5. Meanwhile, a falling saving rate
(3.8% in September 2026, down 1.4pp y/y) suggests households are
drawing on their savings to offset price rises. A large share of the po -
pulation is therefore excluded from the growth that has been observed.
Beyond wages, there is the question of employment prospects.
The unemployment rate remains low and consistent with full employ-

ment. Yet the labour market looks stagnant: low firing despite repeated
shocks and low hiring despite solid growth. Payroll growth (an average
of 68,000 per month since January) is well below that of the previous
decade (nearly 200,000 per month between 2011 and 2019 6). Com-
bined with a workforce that is no longer growing due to an ageing
population and a restrictive immigration policy, this is enough to keep
the unemployment rate low and the labour market looking healthy.

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.40 / 2 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
But households are likely to be suffering from a lack of opportunities,
and consequently from a lack of prospects for wage growth that would
help them to increase their purchasing power. The Conference Board’s
labour differential, a gauge of perceived job availability, says as much:
outside the pandemic, it is at its lowest since 2015.
Housing is the area that has deteriorated the most. The rise in

mortgage rates that began in 2022, from under 4% to over 7%, has
dampened the property market. This is true on the demand side, as
expected, but also on the supply side. The sharp rise in interest rates
is deterring homeowners with low-interest mortgages from selling, as
they would have to borrow again at current rates. This ‘lock-in effect’
prevents prices from falling despite weaker demand. The lock-in effect
is also affecting tenants, as the decline in the availability of housing is

driving up rents. Housing lies at the heart of the disconnect between
the macroeconomic picture and voters’ perception of it, as they face a
multifaceted rise in the cost of living. That rise is also driven by credit:
monthly repayments on a home of the same value have risen shar -
ply. This additional cost, which inflation figures do not capture7, erodes
households’ purchasing power in the property market.
A CONGRESS POWERLESS ON THE COST OF LIVING

The next Congress will, in all likelihood, have little room for ma -
noeuvre. Neither party is within reach of the thresholds required to
override the filibuster (which allows 41 out of 100 senators to block
a bill from coming to a vote) and the presidential veto (two-thirds of
both chambers).
Affordability is likely to be a key issue in the election, but the winners
will lack the tools to address it. The Democrats’ agenda relies notably
on the reintroduction of schemes that were abolished or scaled back

under the current administration (Affordable Care Act tax credits, Me-
dicaid). Even if Congress passed them, they would be subject to a pre-
sidential veto. There is no question of direct support for households or
a restrictive fiscal policy.
Whether inflation falls will depend on two factors: how the conflict in
Iran unfolds and the effectiveness of monetary policy. The labour mar-
ket should hold its current balance: robust growth (see our EcoFlash)

is supporting demand, whilst failing to alleviate supply-side pressures,
and the unemployment rate could continue to fall. Household senti -
ment will continue to depend on how prices develop. As for housing,
it is unlikely to improve in the short term. With the 10-year Treasury
yield still at 4.8% at the end of 2027 (BNP Paribas forecast), 30-year
mortgage rates would, at best, remain around 7%.
THE OTHER ECONOMIC STAKES OF THE VOTE
Historically, divided governments have brought legislative stability,

which is viewed favourably by the markets. But polarisation has in -
creased in recent years and is likely to feature prominently in the bud-
get vote and the debt-ceiling debate. The debt limit, in particular, could
be reached as early as Q1 2027, depending on the Treasury’s borrowing
requirements. The current administration, meanwhile, has taken major
decisions (on tariffs, immigration and Iran) without involving Congress,
a practice that is likely to continue.

7 Marijn A. Bolhuis, Judd N. L. Cramer, Karl Oskar Schulz, Lawrence H. Summers, ‘The cost of money is part of the cost of living: new evidence on the consumer sentiment
anomaly’, NBER Working Paper, No. 32163, 2024.
Risks to the deficit remain tilted to the upside. According to our base -
line scenario, it is expected to remain stable at 6.3% of GDP in 2027,
but the post-election balance of power could add to the cost of the war
in Iran and rising debt-service costs. In particular, interests could align

in favour of increased spending should the Democrats gain control of
both houses of Congress. Avoiding, or ending, a shutdown could require
a trade-off involving mutual concessions from the presidency on key,
popular aspects of the Democratic agenda (such as healthcare), and
from the Democrats on defence.
The AI sector is exposed to the vote. The economic stakes are high
given how much the associated investment cycle and the wealth effect
contribute to US growth. However, at this stage, the risk is not legisla-

tive. As with the cost of living, sweeping legislation is unlikely, all the
more so with a president holding a pro-AI stance, and who holds the
right of veto. The risk lies rather in sentiment and further out. A Demo-
cratic sweep could lead to increased congressional activism on regu -
lation, on concentration in the AI industry, and on the knock-on effects
on the cost of living (data centres and electricity) and on jobs. Putting
these issues on the agenda could create a climate conducive to tighter

regulation in the medium term and weigh on the sector’s valuations.
Furthermore, the mid-term elections will see 36 governors’ seats up
for election. These governors have extensive powers (regarding autho-
risations and incentives) when it comes to the establishment of data
centres in their states.
Finally, the midterms will matter for monetary policy. The Senate
confirms, by simple majority, the president’s nominees to the Fed’s
Board of Governors. Consequently, should the Senate come under De -

mocratic control, President Trump would need a consensus pick to re-
place Governor Powell from 2028 — or to fill any other seat that might
become vacant during the next Congress.
*****
Whatever the outcome of the election, disinflation and, more broadly,
relief from the cost of living, depend less on Congress than on the
White House and, to a lesser extent, the Fed. The key factor lies in the
resolution of the conflict in Iran, coupled with an effective monetary

policy to enable a sustainable return to price stability.
Anis Bensaidani
anis.bensaidani@bnpparibas.com

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
In November 2025, we introduced Westpac-Now (see here),
a continuously-updated measure of current activity in the
Australian economy. Westpac-Now draws on more than
60 high frequency economic and financial variables, using
advanced statistical techniques to provide a timely pulse

check on economic momentum. It is the first Australian
model to incorporate internal banking data, enhancing its
predictive power.
This note provides the second estimate of GDP growth for the
September quarter 2026, using domestic and international
data released up to and including the September Labour Force
Survey (released 15 October 2026). Updates are published
monthly (see Appendix A for the release schedule).
Westpac Monthly Activity Index: activity falters as

headwinds mount
After showing tentative signs of improvement through the
second half of 2025, the Westpac Monthly Activity Index has
weakened markedly over 2026. The Index moved back into
negative territory in February and has declined further since.
While there were some signs of stabilisation around mid-year,
this proved to be short lived with the Index falling to –0.88 in
September, outside of the pandemic period, the weakest read
since 2013.
The economy is coming under pressure from multiple

fronts, with key headwinds coming from higher interest
rates, renewed disruptions to global energy markets and a
deepening in the housing downturn. Among households,
spending held up surprisingly well through the first half of the
year despite historically weak sentiment, but more recently
Growth momentum on shaky ground
spending outside of fuel has started to cool (see here). At
the same time, the labour market has continued to soften,
with unemployment reaching a cycle high of 4.7% (see here).

Meanwhile, the business sector is also showing signs of
softening, with sentiment deteriorating and conditions holding
at weak levels (see here). Business credit growth remains
a notable exception, although even here there are tentative
signs of easing (see here).
This deterioration is particularly evident in our heatmap.
Negative contributions have become both broader and more
pronounced through 2026, with the weakness intensifying
in August and September. Labour and business condition

indicators are now the largest sources of weakness, while
spending and confidence have also deteriorated materially.
Credit and capacity utilisation indicators have been
comparatively less negative, but there are currently few areas
providing a meaningful offset to the broader softening.
Our indicator heatmap decomposes changes in the Monthly
Activity Index into group-level contributions, allowing us to
assess which components are driving movements in activity.

For ease of interpretation, indicators are grouped into broad
categories (see the Appendix for further details). For example,
the labour market category includes both official ABS Labour
Force Survey outcomes and timelier survey-based measures of
labour demand and job advertisements.
Westpac-Now: Resilient growth outlook slowing not
stalling
Recall that Westpac-Now is a refined indicator that combines
our Monthly Activity Index with advanced econometric

techniques to estimate GDP growth. As previously noted,
the fit is not expected to be exact. Measured GDP includes

Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
components that are not well captured by high-frequency
activity indicators, such as imputed rents for owner-occupiers,
housing-related ownership transfer costs, public and business
inventories, and depreciation.
To bridge monthly and quarterly data, we use a MIDAS

framework, allowing us to combine mixed-frequency inputs
and incorporate partial information for the current quarter
when producing GDP forecasts.
Based on current model estimates, Westpac-Now’s second
estimate for Q3 2026 points to GDP growth of 0.5%qtr
(with a 70% confidence interval of –0.1%qtr to 1.0%qtr)
and 2.1%yr, representing a slight downgrade from the first
estimate of +0.6%qtr but still a step up from the 0.4%qtr
(2.1%yr) outcome recorded in Q2 2026. While aggregate

growth appears to have held up through Q3, the underlying
picture became softer as the quarter progressed. At the
second decimal place the estimate is 0.46%qtr, leaving it
close to rounding down to 0.4%qtr.
With several headwinds now bearing on the economy
simultaneously, there is a growing risk that the slowdown in
activity becomes more pronounced than currently anticipated.
At the same time, the strength of the business investment
upswing provides an important counterweight that should

help cushion the broader slowdown.
Looking ahead, model dynamics suggest growth will tick
down to 0.4%qtr in Q4 2026 before picking up in Q1 2027.
However, these projections remain much more tentative,
given their reliance on projected inputs rather than realised
data. In addition, heightened uncertainty, particularly around
the renewed tensions in the Middle East conflict, means risks
around these forward estimates are larger than usual.
September Quarter data: a softer end to the quarter

Data released in the first month of the September quarter
indicated that the economy has picked up momentum relative
to the previous quarter, with our first nowcast estimating Q3
growth at 0.6%qtr, in line with the 0.6%qtr “pure forecast”
based solely on data available up to June 2026.
However, the flow of data since then shows downside risks to
growth have begun to materialise. Incorporating the August,
followed by the September (to date), data both yielded

nowcasts of 0.5%qtr (with a slight softening at the second
decimal place). This contrasts with Q2 2026, when weakness
emerged early in the quarter, in April, before subsequent data
pointed to stabilisation.
Note that monthly activity estimates are more uncertain
than normal when inflation is shifting. Faster price growth
can cause nominal indicators, such as consumer spending,
to rise more strongly even though ‘real’, inflation-adjusted
spending may be softening (and vice versa). The inclusion

of ‘real’ indicators, such as employment and banking
transaction volumes, helps mitigate some – but not all – of this
uncertainty.
Bottom line:
Westpac-Now points to GDP growth of around 0.5%qtr
(2.1%yr) in Q3 2026, suggesting aggregate growth held up
reasonably well through the quarter, although the flow of
data points to softer momentum as the quarter progressed.
The substantial upswing in business investment remains
an important source of support, helping to offset mounting

weakness elsewhere in the economy.
Looking ahead, the accumulation of headwinds is expected to
see growth slow further towards year-end, with Westpac-Now
currently pointing to growth of around 0.4%qtr in Q4 2026.
Downside risks around the outlook have increased, but for now
our central case remains one of slower growth rather than a
material downturn.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

Appendix A: Westpac-Now Release Schedule (2026)
Going forward, Westpac Now will be published on the Friday following each ABS Labour Force Survey (LFS) release, at 11:30am AEDT/AEST (Canberra time).
Each release incorporates the latest labour market data alongside other high frequency indicators and provides an updated nowcast for quarterly GDP growth.

As the quarter progresses, each update should be interpreted as a progressively more complete estimate of that quarter’s economic performance.
Release date (Friday) LFS reference month Quarter focus Interpretation
20 November 2026 October 2026 Q3 2026 – final estimate Near complete read on Q3 activity
18 December 2026 November 2026 Q4 2026 – first estimate Initial assessment of Q4 momentum

• 美國多州出現暫停資料中心建

本。
《部分電力股評價將更看重監管可預測性》
在2026年美國期中選舉涉及的36場州長選戰中，已
有20州出現暫停資料中心建設或許可的政策訴求 。
電價、用水與基礎設施成本的分攤爭議 ，正改寫各
州爭取AI投資的方向 ，資金可能從監管混沌的地區 ，
加速流向規則較為明確的州 。德州為這輪監管分化
中最值得關注 、也最棘手的地區 。德州電網接入申
請佇列已成長到約 474吉瓦，其中約九成來自資料

中心，而德州州長已宣布暫停核准新的資料中心接
入州內電網，9月底環境委員會的暫停令進一步擴及
自備電源的離網專案 ，意味企業即使自己發電 ，也
未必能繞過許可關卡。
相較之下，在大規模用電需求湧入前 ，先把監管制
度架好的州或許更具產業前景 。懷俄明、明尼蘇達、
愛荷華、威斯康辛 、內華達、南卡羅來納與田納西
州為潛在的資金承接者 。明尼蘇達已立法防止大客
戶把成本轉嫁給一般用戶 ，科羅拉多的大負荷費率
方案則要求至少15年合約、80%最低計費需求與提
前退出費 ，把部分退出風險交還給客戶 。電力股的
投資邏輯正在轉向 ，未來部分公用事業股的評價分
化，將從過去單純看需求規模 ，轉向更看重獲利品
質與監管可預測性。
《AI資料中心電力需求仍為近期財報重點 ，但焦點
轉向如何平衡供應瓶頸與監管成本》
隨著3Q26財報陸續披露，美國公用事業公司對AI資
料中心用電需求普遍釋出極為樂觀且迫切的長線成
長展望。以FirstEnergy為例，受惠於毗鄰北弗吉尼
亞數據中心走廊的優勢 ，西弗吉尼亞州等地的資料

中 心 相 關 負 載 需 求 激 增 137% 達到 4.3 吉瓦 。
American Electric Power其累積的增量負載承諾已
飆升至56吉瓦，半年內暴增一倍 。但公用事業高層
的態度在積極擴產中也帶有審慎風險控管 ，為了避
免新建發電廠及電網升級的費用侵蝕一般用戶權益 ，
業者正重新設計合約 ，要求科技公司簽署最低電費
與信用擔保條款 。整體而言 ，公用事業財報反映出
資料中心用電需求並未降溫 ，但產業焦點已從 「如
何接到訂單 」轉向「如何平衡供應瓶頸與監管成本 」
的階段。

智原 3035 TT  ( 電子–半導體 ) 
緩步前進，待評價面更具吸引力 
投資與評價 
1. 2026 年雖子公司 MCU 業務挹注，但受產能限制及認列時程影響，預估
營收將年減 26.2%。 
2. 遞延專案將於明年貢獻外 ，預估有三至四個先進製程專案進入 T ape-out
階段，但仍將面臨供應鏈成本壓力，預估 2027 營收年增 25.2%。 
3. 預估智原 2026/2027 年 EPS 為 3.27/5.39 元，考量先進封裝/製程為主
要增長動能，營收佔比高之成熟製程維持穩健下，以歷史本益比區間中
間偏上 35 倍為評價基礎，目標價 188 元(35X FY27E EPS)，股價未具顯
著吸引力，故由買進轉為中立投資建議。 
重點更新 
受限封裝產能限制及專案認列時程影響，預估 2026 年營收將年減 26.2% 
子公司 MCU 業務優於研究部先前預期，全年成長翻倍，主要受惠成熟製程
效能提升下 ，高階 MCU 配合演算法能夠完成客戶所需特定功能。雖 MCU 表
現亮眼，但高階封裝業務則受到封測產能與材料供應限制，目前透過供應鏈
協調逐步改善，加上部分案件認列時程遞延，推故將至明年上半年認列，使
整體營收表現不如研究部預期，目前累計 Design Win 訂單金額中，美國成
為 NRE 業務最大貢獻市場，亦成功取得美國客戶的三星 2nm GAA 雲端 AI
設計專案。整體 2026 年營收研究部預估將年減 26.2%，達 132.9 億元。 
2027 年先進製程/封裝為主要動能，但評價未具顯著吸引力 
除前述 26 年部分專案時程遞延至 1H27 認列外，預估上半年有三至四個先
進製程相關專案進入 T ape-out 階段，NRE 將依照合約里程碑逐步認列。其
中 2nm GAA 專案，研究部推估 NRE 合約金額約為 5000~6000 萬美元，首
筆收入亦於 1H27 依專案進度認列，後續仍有設計、流片及量產階段的長期
營收貢獻。矽電容取得 MLCC 客戶委託開發，1H27 進入 tape-out。光通訊
方面，光模組有相關MCU 客戶，光交換機相關專案執行中，CXL 預計 2H27
量產。MCU 則將透過子公司雅特力既有產品及新產品量產，維持穩定出貨，
但 2027 年仍存在供應鏈成本壓力，目前晶圓代工廠已發出自第一季起調漲
價格的通知，記憶體及封測供給也預期持續偏緊。整體而言 2027 年營收預
估年增 25.2%，達 166.3 億元，以評價面考量，股價未具顯著吸引力。

訪談內容 
先進製程及封裝仍是重點 
智原未來技術發展將採取成熟製程完整平台化、先進製程依客戶需求投入的雙軌策略，核心方向是整合  IP、ASIC 
設計、晶圓代工及先進封裝，提供客戶一站式設計服務。成熟製程方面，公司已建立 40、28、22 及 14nm 相關 
IP 平台，並利用既有 14nm 技術經驗與設計資產，推進 12nm IP 開發，以降低研發成本、縮短平台建置時間。對
於更先進的製程，則依客戶需求及投資報酬率選擇性投入，避免無效的研發支出。

先進製程方面，最具代表性的進展是與三星晶圓代工合作的 2nm GAA 雲端 AI 專案。該案整合 PCIe Gen6 等先進 
IP、客製化 3D DRAM 堆疊、Hybrid Bonding 及 TSV 等技術，並結合智原既有 2.5D 封裝與 OSAT 供應鏈經驗。
客戶原先規劃使用 4nm，後因效能及功耗需求升級至 2nm，產品涉及高精度 64-bit 浮點科學運算。

Edge AI 方面，智原將結合雅特力標準MCU 與自身客製化 SoC、NPU 設計能力，涵蓋低功耗 AIoT 及高效能邊緣
推論市場。雅特力 Edge AI 相關營收目前已占其整體營收約 25%，並參與 Physical AI 相關產業聯盟。 
其他新興布局包括與聯電關注矽電容製程應用、光通訊及光交換器接收端設計，以及CXL 控制晶片。其中，矽電容
相關專案預計 2027 年陸續量產，CXL 專案則 2027 年下半年量產，整體策略是透過跨廠商技術整合，將新取得的
Design Win 逐步轉化為長期量產營收及 IP 權利金收入。

以目前公司策略來講，14nm 以上製程，主要與聯電、三星合作，在 12nm 以下，則以 Intel 與三星為核心夥伴。
亦與 GlobalFoundries 今年正式建立合作關係。

表一、智原修正預估與前次預估比較

資料來源：國泰證期研究部預估

智原 ESG 政策 
智原科技主要提供特定用途的積體電路(ASIC)設計服務及矽智財(SIP)，公司名列全球前 15 大 SIP 矽智 
財供應商、全球前 50 大 ASIC 設計服務供應商，產業類別歸類在半導體行業中。依據專門為投資人設 
計的永續揭露框架 SASB 準則，半導體行業關注重點為下列幾項：溫室氣體排放、能源管理、水和廢水 
管理、廢棄物和危險材料管理、員工健康與安全、員工多元化和包容性、產品設計與生命週期管理、材 
料採購與效率。由於智原科技本身並無工廠，研究部將篇幅著重在產品設計面，並因員工對於 IC 設計 
公司而言是相當重要的資產，故員工相關議題也會著重於下描述，其餘溫室氣體及能/資源管理等則於 
後簡述。

1. 產品設計管理： 
在原料取得與生產製造部分，智原依據歐盟廢電子電機設備中危害物質禁用指令（RoHS）及歐盟化學政策
（REACH）建立綠色生產供應鏈，推行綠色採購，並制定【綠色環保政策及作業程序】及【環境物質管理作業辦法】 ，
要求所有生產供應商必須簽署環境物質不使用承諾書, 禁用危害環境物料；並且要求供應商以及上一級供應商須對有害
物質進行原材料監控與製程監控管理，並提供第三方的檢測報告以確保符合綠色產品以及國際法規。智原科技所提供
之 A S I C 產品， 符合 Eco-design 有害化學物質評估不含鎘 Cadmium(Cd)及鹵素(氯、溴) Halogen (Cl, Br)。生態
設計(Eco-design)指令中，要求能耗產品的製造商在設計階段時，有義務減少產品的耗能和負面的環境影響。該指令
的主要目的是減少能源使用、提高能源應用效率及降低對環境的衝擊，智原 PowerSlash™ IP 能在 0.81V 至 1. 32V 廣
域電壓下運作。特有的加速模式（Turbo Mode）可在額定頻率下減少 MCU 核心約 40%的動態功耗。

2. 員工健康與安全： 
智原採 TOSHMS 台灣職業安全衛生管理系統之要求，定期檢視執行成果與缺失檢討，以預防 
及降低工安發生率。依據勞動部職業安全衛生署之職業災害失能認定，若排除上下班交通意外事故，智原於 2021 年皆
無因職業所引起之疾病、傷害、失能或死亡。針對零星同仁上下班途中發生之交通意外事故，統計資料如圖一。智原
藉由安全衛生教育訓練活動，建構安全職場文化、營造健康職場氛圍、強化安衛管理目標，保障同仁安全，定期舉辦
相關職業安全訓練，針對不同類別工作場域會遇到的情況，提供不同類型的安全衛生教育訓練，相關受訓人數以及時
數如圖二。

圖一、員工意外事故比例

資料來源：智原、國泰證期研究部整理

3. 員工多元化以及包容性： 
智原致力於多元化招聘策略，積極延攬全球各地專業人才，在全球各地建立符合未來需求的 
人才庫及研發中心。全球員工來自台灣、中國、美國、加拿大、日本、韓國、印度、越南、 
馬來西亞、新加坡、亞美尼亞等國。據台灣地區《身心障礙者權益保障法》第 38 條規定，企業進用具有就業能力之身
心障礙者人數，不得低於員工總人數 1%。其中，進用重度以上身心障礙者，每進用 1 人得以 2 人核計。為保障身心
障礙人士的工作權益，智原積極配合政府政策，依法雇用身心障礙人士共 4 名 (包含重度 2 位、中度 1 位、輕度 1
位)。加權雇用比率符合法令規定，顯示智原對於弱勢族群之照顧，提供其公平就業機會，保障其就業權益。

4. 水和廢水管理： 
智原公司內無生產製程作業，故無工業廢水、工業廢氣、毒氣、化學品。產品生產委由專業代工廠製造，能源需求多
為空調、照明及伺服器設備用電等一般民生需求。用水 100%來自台灣自來水公司，主要為一般生活用水，無製程用
水。2022 年總用水量為 18,512.611 度，較 2021 年增加 276.369 度。主要原因為 2021 年 5~7 月因 COVID-19 疫情
影響，實施分流上班，致辦公室用水量減少，2022 年疫情趨緩回歸正常上班型態，智原持續推動各項節水措施與宣
導，減少及管控水資源消耗。

圖三、智原 2020~2022 年用水量統計

資料來源:智原、國泰證期研究部整理

5. 溫室氣體排放： 
智原於 2022 年 10 月 14 日完成總部大樓屋頂建置太陽能發電系統自建自用，裝置容量 89.70KWp，產生電力供
給廠內使用，統計至 2022 年 12 月 31 日產電量 20,122 度，減碳量 10.242 噸 COe，預估年平均發電量約
93,731 度(年減碳量達 47.709 噸 COe)。未來持續規劃節能減碳措施及執行，中長期視後續減碳狀況評估綠電採
購，確保達成 2050 年溫室氣體淨零排放目標。

圖四、智原 2020~2022 溫室氣體排放總量 
資料來源:智原、國泰證期研究部整理

投資評等定義

電子製造業 ESG 評等分配圖

瑞昱 2379 TT  ( 電子上游–IC 設計 ) 
網通規格逐步推進，待評價面更具吸引力 
投資與評價 
1. PC 表現雖較為疲弱，但受惠基建升級與高頻寬解決方案的挹注下，網通
產品需求優於預期，預估 26 年營收年增 21.8% 
2. 規格升級仍將為主要驅動力，AI 產品亦添柴火，但推估傳統應用在高基
期下成長性有限，預估 27 年營收年增 9.7%。 
3. 預估瑞昱 2026/2027 年 EPS 為 31.91/38.15 元，考量成長動能及金融
環境不確定性，以 27 年獲利預估乘上本益比區間上緣 20 倍為評價基
礎，目標價 760 元(20X FY27E EPS)，股價尚未具顯著吸引力，維持中
立投資評等。 
重點更新 
網通產品維持韌性，表現略優於預期，預估 2026 營收年增 21.8% 
網通需求受惠基礎建設持續升級與高頻寬解決方案的挹注，其中乙太網路隨
路由器、中小企業網路及伺服器採用擴大而成長，網管型交換器在企業、中
小企業及伺服器控制層應用持續提升市占 ， 與非網管型產品的差距逐漸縮小 。
Wi-Fi 方面，Wi-Fi 7 在 PC 與路由器的滲透率，預估今年約達 30%，在車用
乙太網路採用持續穩定擴大，今年營收亦呈穩定成長態勢。但在消費性電子
方面，因 DRAM 與 CPU 等零組件持續漲價推升零售價格，削弱消費性與入
門級購買力，提前拉貨效應亦趨緩，使得PC 表現研究部預估年減約一成，另
外電視出貨在年中世足賽備貨後放緩，零組件成本壓力也持續存在 。綜合考
量下，整體預估瑞昱 2026 營收年增 21.8%達 1494.6 億元。 
2027 年規格升級及 AI 為主要驅動力，但評價仍不具吸引力 
Wi-Fi 方面，Wi-Fi 7 的滲透率，預估 2027 年將達 40%，新一代 Wi-Fi 8 開
發進度順利，預計 2H27 量產，乙太網路與 10G PON 採用擴大，帶動產品
組合改善，高階網管型交換器預估也將取得更多 design win。目前伺服器產
品涵蓋管理連接與周邊功能，包括乙太網路與交換器、 SSD 控制器、 I/O 
bridge 及 USB hub 控制器，並以此為基礎進入更高速的伺服器互連，推估
2027 年營收佔比將達一成水準 。100G 光通訊解決方案正進行認證，預期
2027 年放量更明顯，400G 與 800G 解決方案開發則穩定進行，預估 2H27
推出，PC 則預估呈現持平表現。整體而言，預估 27 年營收年增 9.7%，達
1639.1 億元，以評價面來考量，目前股價仍尚未具顯著吸引力。

資料來源：台灣指數公司，國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 2

訪談內容 
網通產品規格升級+AI 將為長期成長動能 
2026 年 Wi-Fi 7 在 PC/NB 市場滲透率達 30%，約為 2025 年的兩倍，高階商用機型導入最積
極，Wi-Fi 聯盟目標 2027 年 6 月前完成認證測試計畫，並於 2027 年 9 月正式啟動 Wi-Fi 8 認
證。正積極與客戶進行Wi-Fi 8 design-in，預期產品 2H27 開始放量，客戶產品將在正式認證後
於 2028 年更廣泛上市。Wi-Fi 8 在 Wi-Fi 7 的基礎上著重實際使用環境中的極高可靠度，以及更
低、更穩定的延遲，預估將成為廣泛採用且生命周期長的平台。

車用乙太網路在新車生產的滲透率已超過 50%，全球汽車出貨持平，成長並非依賴車輛出貨增
加，而是來自單車乙太網路埠數結構性增加、持續打入新車廠客戶，以及穩定的 design win 動
能。隨車廠電子電機架構演進，需求自然轉向更高密度的安全關鍵解決方案，產品路線圖與此趨
勢一致，正擴充更高整合度、更多埠數的車用交換器，並開發先進閘道控制器，以因應未來軟體
定義汽車的需求。

另外 AI ASIC 機會目前處於與客戶接觸的初期階段 ， 基礎為高速連接與介面IP 的優勢 。 邊緣AI(含
地端伺服器與邊緣伺服器)是推動規格升級、提升單機價值的結構性機會，而非僅依賴PC 需求復
甦的成長動能。邊緣 AI 對更高頻寬、更低延遲、更佳功耗效率與更豐富多模態互動的需求，可
為產品組合創造更高價值的解決方案。

供應端部分預期 2027 年改善將較明顯，工程端也導入 design for availability 原則，在可行範
圍內降低對受限物料的依賴。成本漲幅已超過可合理吸收的程度，與客戶逐案討論價格調整。

### Page 3

瑞昱 ESG 政策 
瑞昱主要從事 IC 設計，產業類別歸類在半導體行業中，依據專門為投資人設計的永續揭露框架 SASB
準則，半導體行業關注重點為下列幾項：溫室氣體排放、能源管理、水和廢水管理、廢棄物和危險材料
管理、員工健康與安全、員工多元化和包容性、產品設計與生命週期管理、材料採購與效率。由於瑞昱
為 IC 設計公司，研究部將篇幅著重在產品設計面，並因員工對於IC 設計公司而言是相當重要的資產，
故員工相關議題也會著重於下描述，其餘溫室氣體排放及產品設計與生命週期管理等則於後簡述。

1. 員工健康與安全： 
瑞昱重視及關心同仁的身體健康與生活狀態，以優於法令之規定，每年定期實施免費健檢服務。對於健
康檢查結果為重大 ( 含輕度 ) 異常同仁，依異常項目、病情及個人健康情形，於複檢前、後分別安排
廠醫諮詢及衛教說明，持續追蹤健檢結果。與新竹臺大分院新竹醫院合作之健康諮詢服務，每月安排 9 
場次駐廠醫師健康諮詢服務，每場次由專業醫師提供  3 小時諮詢服務，2022 年度共計服務 384 人
次。

圖 1 歷年健檢人數統計

資料來源：瑞昱半導體、國泰證期研究部整理

2. 員工多元化和包容性： 
2022 年瑞昱全集團全球員工人數為 6,828 人，海外員工註人數佔總 員工人數約 36.9%，其中 72% 
以上同仁擁有碩士、博士學歷。瑞昱半導體以臺灣為主要營運據點， 臺灣地區員工人數為 4,943 人，
其中正式員工人數 4,826 人，約聘員工人數 117 人，男性員工所佔 比例約 80.46%、女性員工所佔
比例約 19.54%，其中 77% 以上同仁擁有碩士、博士學歷。在 2022 年所聘僱的人力中，無論在正
式員工、男性員工、女性員工等類別指標，皆有明顯成長。 
圖 2 近三年員工人數

資料來源：瑞昱半導體、國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 4

3. 溫室氣體排放： 
瑞昱在 2022 年溫室氣體排放共計 26,462.8729 tCO2e ，其中直接排放(範疇 1)為 808.4856 tCO2e，
分別為固定排放源(緊急發電機之柴油)、移動排放源(公務車之汽油)及逸散排放源(冷媒、化糞池、滅火
器)，瑞昱因無生產製程，故無製程排放源；間接排放(範疇 2)為 25654.3873 tCO2e，主要來源為外購
電力，占整體排放量 96.9%。

圖 3 溫室氣體排放量、強度

資料來源：瑞昱半導體、國泰證期研究部整理

4. 水和廢水管理： 
瑞昱於 2022 年持續成功開發出多項新一代產品，在各種應用功耗 （瓦數） 表現上比前一代產品功耗減
少、面積也縮小，在長時間及大量傳輸時更具顯著降低耗能之效果，充份體現節能與對環境之友善，並
帶給終端用戶更佳的使用體驗。

圖 4 產品設計成效

資料來源：瑞昱半導體、國泰證期研究部整理

熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《地緣風險衝擊中東信用基本面 ，但緩衝能力仍佳 》
中東戰事持續外擴 ，除了美伊相互襲擊使得荷姆茲
海峽情勢依然緊張外 ，葉門胡塞武裝也對沙烏地阿
拉伯密集發動攻勢 ，柴油出口因此受阻 ，能源價格
處高位不墜 ，布蘭特原油 10月以來便維持在 100美
元/桶之上。過往中東國家往往是高油價的受惠者，
但受到能源生產和運輸中斷衝擊 ，國際貨幣基金10
月下調2026年中東及中亞地區經濟成長率預估值 1
個百分點至衰退，沙烏地阿拉伯更預估將衰退 3.3%。

資產 4Q26殖利率目標價 投資展望

新興主要貨幣主權債指數 6.70% 較高的殖利率及穩健基本面將使其在利率波動時更為穩健
新興主要貨幣企業債指數 6.64% A~BB級企業相對穩健
新興投資級債指數 5.55% 亞洲企業債供給有限支撐買氣，中東良好債信吸引力仍佳
新興非投資級債指數 7.50% BB級具升等潛力，拉美非投資級債可望因油價上漲而受惠
新興當地貨幣債指數 6.80% 風險溢酬反應貨幣政策較差情境，升息預期淡化後殖利率下行
人民幣債指數 2.20% 景氣不均衡復甦，寬鬆貨幣政策為主要支撐力
亞洲當地貨幣債指數 4.60% 亞洲國家貨幣政策觀望，短線維持中性看法

重點摘要 沙烏地阿拉伯 (以下簡稱沙國 )雖極力避免直接捲入
戰爭，但仍遭受衝突外溢效應波及 。戰爭不僅導致
沙國大幅增加補貼 ，軍事和安全支出以及融資費用
上升均加大財政支出 ，因此，即便油價上漲抵消產
量下降的影響而使石油收入高於預期，沙國2Q26預
算赤字佔 GDP比重仍達 7.1%，遠高於年度目標的
3.3%。儘管近期公布的 2027年預算計畫 (PBS)目標
是將預算赤字佔 GDP比率從2026年的4.9%(沙國官
方最新預估 )縮小到2027年的3.6%，但若戰爭長時
間 延 續， 此 目 標 或 難 以 達 成， 目 前 估 計2027 年
17420億里亞爾的債務目標亦將輕易被超越 。不過
即使如此 ，沙國目前負債占 GDP比重僅約 35%，

2027年市場最悲觀預期也大約40%左右，仍是低於
全球新興國家均值約80%的水準。
中東另一主要發債國卡達對天然氣出口依賴度高 ，
天然氣出口受阻也使得國際機構普遍預估卡達 2026
年經濟衰退10%以上、幾乎是2010年以來最重，所
幸卡達2026年以前累積巨額經常帳盈餘及外匯儲備
作為緩衝，即使在今年3月外部環境面臨極大壓力下，
卡達仍有 199億里亞爾的經常帳盈餘 ，累積過去四
季經常帳占 GDP比重仍達 13.5%，現今外匯存底足
以彌補3月的貿易逆差，並可維持４年以上。這些數
據都顯示戰爭對中東國家的經濟及對外償債能力確
實已帶來短線壓力 ，但過去累積的龐大緩衝使其具
備良好因應能力 ，信用違約交換 (CDS)目前並未因
定價油氣供應長期中斷而大幅攀升 ，今年雖持續發
債但債務壓力並不高 ，IMF更預期2027年中東國家
經濟將成長6.3%。然中東國家對能源出口依賴度仍
高，能源出口是否長期受阻則是後續觀察重點。
重點摘要
• 沙烏地阿拉伯負債占 GDP比
重約 35%低於全球新興國家

均值 ，2027年預算目標為將

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 《經濟強勁帶來的高利率無損新興債利差穩步收斂 》
財政收支並非驅動新興主權信用利差的唯一因素 ，
總體經濟環境 、市場流動性均牽動利差走勢 ，因此
雖然自7月以來沙烏地阿拉伯等中東國家信用利差又
再擴大，但也大概在3年均值附近並不算高。以歷史
經驗來看，BB級主權國家利差通常對財政惡化程度
反應較大 ，可能是因為該評等僅低於投資等級一級 ，
財政變化容易牽動投資人對升等至投資級的預期與
投資部位的調整 ，而在投資等級的主權國家中 ，則
以匈牙利和波蘭這些債務負擔較重的主權債利差較
易受財政問題影響。
油價和利率是近期2項重要觀察變數，也牽動利差走
勢，不過由於新興主權債指數中約5成的組成為大宗
商品出口國、3成為石油出口國，反而能受惠油價上

漲，油價上漲時與利差走勢敏感度也較油價下跌時
低；然而 ，更重要的是高油價對通膨和政策應對的
二階影響 ，將導致高利率長時間延續的可能性並最
終衝擊實體經濟 ，進一步限制石油出口國利差收窄 。
以長期趨勢來看 ，利率水準的絕對值高低不必然影
響新興債利差 ，利率走勢反而和信用利差呈現小幅
負相關，利率走升反映的若是強勁經濟成長而非能
源供給衝擊 ，此時往往能伴隨信用利差收窄 ，反而
需留意的是利率波動放大及利率的大幅重新定價 ，
這才是牽動利差波動加大的主因 。目前總體經濟環
境部分或可與2004~2007年相比擬，當時在強勁成
長的背景下利率長時間在4%~5.3%高位震盪，但新
興主要貨幣主權債利差仍可收斂至 134點(此波低點
189點)，若經濟成長能在現今高油價和高利率環境
下持續強勁，利差則可望保持穩定。
重點摘要
• BB級主權債及債務負擔沈重
的投資級主權債利差較容易
受財政問題干擾
• 新興主權債指數中約 5成為石
油出口國 ，降低油價上漲對
利差走勢的敏感性
3
即使油價上升，新興債利差與油價也少見正相關

重點摘要 《景氣及企業獲利續成長 ，企業債信改善趨勢延續 》
三大信評機構持續認可新興市場四大區域企業信用
評等的改善，3Q26新興市場企業債淨升等規模大幅
提高至760億美元，其中獲上調規模達到910億美元、
創下2025年第四季以來的最高水平 ，也已是連續第
5個季度改善。
3Q26新興市場4大區域評等調升數量均高於下調 ，
其中以亞洲改善動能最為顯著 ，主要貢獻來自於亞
洲金融業 ，全年升評 /降評比率已來到 1.8倍，逆轉
2019年降評多於升評的劣勢；拉美企業仍是新興市
場中違約率最高的區域 ，降評債券數量與其他區域
比也確實較高 ，但降評企業家數已是過去 10年來最
低，加上阿根廷主權債信獲上調帶動企業債 (主要是
國有企業)信評調升，拉美航空業者如南美航空集團

(LATAM Airlines)在油價維持高位環境下仍於3Q26
獲信評機構調升至 BB+，在企業債信分化環境下仍
有亮點 ，因此第三季拉美企業淨評級上調規模達
370億美元，反而創下2025年第三季以來的最佳季
度表現 ，全年共有 234家拉美企業信評得調升 。新
歐中東地區債信今年算是少數債信惡化的區域 ，很
大部分是受到戰爭及高油價影響所致 ，不過奈及利
亞和南非等主權評級上調仍帶來正向效果 ，梯瓦製
藥(Teva)評等更是上調至投資等級，帶來了120億美
元的貢獻，部分抵消地緣政治風險帶來的影響。
強勁的獲利成長持續支持新興企業穩健基本面 ，有
限的違約率輔以韌性十足的總經環境 、以及活躍但
不過度集中的初級市場 ，均是支持債信改善的原因 ，
預期在企業獲利仍看好的情境下 ，第四季企業債信
仍將朝正向發展。

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要 《聯準會升息年 ，新興債雖有波動但正報酬機率高 》
觀察過往聯準會升息年度 ，利率溫和走升機率相對
較高，新興債市雖然多會受到升息壓力而出現一波
跌勢，但全年度仍有較大機率收紅 ，1996年以來僅
有2018、2022這兩個升息年度新興債市全年收黑 。
不過2022年新興債市重挫很大一部分原因來自於聯
準會在短短一年時間內將基準利率自0％升至4.25%，
這種猛爆性升息與目前所預設的風險管理式的溫和
升息環境有所不同；另外 ，2018年聯準會雖只升息
四碼，但土耳其及阿根廷因美元稀缺引發流動性風
險，這2國經常帳赤字占 GDP比重當時均高於 5%，
對外融資壓力上升的同時進而爆發金融危機 。然而
現階段新興國家經常帳已普遍改善 ，土耳其經常帳

赤字已降至 2%~3%，阿根廷更轉為順差 ，基本面
與2018年有很大的差異。若扣除這兩段期間，新興
債市在聯準會升息期間波動雖可能放大 ，但在貨幣
政策方向更趨明朗後 ，走勢便會止穩反彈 ，全年平
均報酬率仍有10%以上。
至於新興市場企業債部分 ，過去因升息所造成的跌
勢約是主權債的一半 ，主要是因為企業債發行天期
多較主權要來得短 ，加上該期間獲利動能強勁 ，因
此在利率上升時期表現往往相對主權債更具韌性 。
儘管利率走勢仍存不確定性，但最終若能穩定在5%
左右，預估新興企業債全年總報酬仍可維持在3%，
除非未來出現更大的外部衝擊 ，如利率持續大幅上
升引發對經濟成長受損的擔憂 ，或AI驅動的成長模
式被重新評價 ，否則對新興債市的看法仍將維持正
向，新興主要貨幣主權債在這樣的大環境下 ，短線
雖相對承壓，但後續反彈力道也可望大於企業債。
• 新興主要貨幣主權債在聯準
會升息年度正報酬機率近8成
• 新興企業債相對抗跌 ，公債
利率若穩定在5%左右，全年
總報酬仍可維持在3%
5

重點摘要
1
分析師／林亞瑱
研究範疇／歐洲總經政策與市場分析
《財政與政治不確定性壓抑估值，歐股延續震盪》
市場對法國財政可持續性以及其可能產生的外溢擔
憂升溫，牽動十月以來歐股走勢震盪，銀行股跌勢
較重，所幸食品飲料與健護等防禦型產業 、能源類
股上揚支撐歐股收紅。評估目前情勢屬法國財政信
心危機，而非歐洲主權風險全面升溫；融資成本上
升對歐洲企業帶來壓力，然整體槓桿可控，財務體
質脆弱的企業面臨較大風險。隨著預算協商進行、
財政擔憂短線難消除，歐央12月利率會議可能再升
息一碼，公債殖利率預估將維持高位，加上中東地
緣風險未除，將對市場情緒與歐股評價帶來壓力 ，
預估歐股短線將延續震盪走勢，維持中立評等。

重點摘要
2
• 法國財政疑慮致法德利差大
幅擴大。
• 預估現任總理勒克努仍有機
會透過援引 《憲法 》第49.3
條於年底前通過一份財政整
頓力度較有限的預算案 ，然
而，即使預算案過關 ，也難
扭轉法國債務攀升趨勢。
資料來源：野村，2026/10/7，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10。
2027年預算提案樽節開支，但將面對政治角力
《法國財政疑慮、政治僵局與社會動盪導致法德利
差擴大，明年大選前政治風險溢酬預估維持高檔》
十月份法國公債殖利率大幅上升 ，反映法國政治
局勢分裂 、財政整頓前景不明 ，加上明年總統大
選恐由極右派勝出 ，市場對法國財政可持續性的
疑慮升高 ，法國十年期公債殖利率 10月下旬一度
突破5%，創2002年7月以來新高。

近期法國各地爆發大規模抗議活動 ，亦對市場帶
來衝擊 。法國政治與財政風險升溫 ，反而吸引資
金轉向德國公債避險 ，加上近期金融條件趨緊 、
市場對歐央升息的預期相較九月底降溫 ，推動德
國十年期公債殖利率自高檔回落 ，法德利差大幅
擴大 、10/22一度突破 170個基點 ，創2011年11
月歐債危機以來新高(統計至10/26)。
針對2027年預算 ，法國政府提出 430億歐元財政
整頓方案，目標將財政赤字占 GDP比率自2026年
的5.4%降至2027年的5%，措施包括凍結公部門
薪資、限制每月超過1,260歐元的退休金通膨調整
幅度、延長針對大型企業的特別稅但縮小適用範
圍等。反對黨已對多項措施表達反對 ，若不採取
任何樽節措施 ，2027年法國財政赤字占 GDP比率
預估將達6.5%。由於大選在即 ，從近期極右派國
民聯盟釋出的意向來看 ，雖不支持預算案 ，卻也
不願在大選前引發政治危機 ，預估現任總理勒克
努仍有機會透過援引 《憲法》第49.3條於年底前
通過一份財政整頓力度較有限的預算案 。然而，

即使預算案過關，也難扭轉法國債務攀升趨勢。

重點摘要
3
近來融資成本 上升進一步加重法國的債務負擔 。
信評機構 惠譽(2026/10/12)上調利息支出預測 ，
預估2026年至2028年間，利息支出將累積佔GDP
的0.8%。利息支出占GDP比率預估將由2026年約
2.5%升至 20 28年約3.3%，2030年進一步升至
3.8%，反映高融資成本對財政的壓力將逐步顯現 。
利息支出增加將壓縮其他公共支出及財政空間 。
若赤字改善幅度不如預期 ，債務比率恐持續上升 。
2027年總統大選亦將牽動法國財政政策與債市走
向。法國總統大選預計於 2027年4至5月舉行，第
一輪投票訂於 4/18，若無候選人取得過半選票將
於5/2進行第二輪投票。最新民調顯示，極右派國
民聯盟候選人勒龐在首輪支持度領先 ，超過三成；

極左派法國不屈黨候選人梅朗雄近期支持度上升 ，
引發市場擔憂將發生沒有中間派候選人進入第二
輪決選的不利情境。
梅朗雄主張擴張性財政政策 ，對市場最為不利 ，
勝選機率較高的勒龐 10/6提出財政整頓方案並且
主張建立一個可信的債務穩定框架 ，試圖安撫市
場對法國財政前景的疑慮 。在面對市場壓力下 ，
勒龐表達更具財政紀律的立場有助稍稍緩和財政
擴張的擔憂 ，但其財政整頓方案的政策假設與具
體實施存在不確定性 ，加上大選後很可能緊接著
進行國會改選 ，政治版圖維持分裂狀態不利財政
改革。
資料來源：Fitch，2026/10/12，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10。
融資成本上升將加重法國債務負擔
利息支出占GDP比率預測(%)

務負擔。
• 2027年法國將舉行總統大選
及緊接著可能進行國會改選 ，
加大市場對極端黨派若勝選
將導致財政鬆動的擔憂。

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要
4
整體而言 ，財政和政治不確定性預估將令法德利
差維持在高位 ，下檔情境為若 2027年預算案無法
於年底前通過 ，恐將面臨更大的財政惡化與市場
壓力，十年期法德利差恐有進一步擴大的風險。
《目前情勢屬法國財政信心危機，而非歐洲主權風
險全面升溫》
儘管投資人對法國債券要求更高的風險補償 ，但
近期法國公債標售仍有足夠的認購需求 。除此之
外，目前法德利差擴大對整體歐洲市場的外溢影
響尚屬可控 ，多數歐洲國家相對德國利差雖受牽
動近期亦出現較明顯的擴大 ，但利差水準仍可控 。
以政府債務占 GDP比重較高的義大利來看 ，十年
期義德利差 10/22一度升至 124個基點 ，回升至
2025年4月水準，但仍遠低於 2011年歐債危機時

期。因此，目前情勢仍偏向法國自身的財政信心
危機，而非歐洲主權信用風險全面升溫。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2024年來至2026/10/21，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10。

重點摘要
5
• 10月歐元區 PMI維持擴張但
成長放緩 ，通膨壓力面臨上
行風險 。不過 ，未出現二輪
通膨效應 ，預期歐央連續升
息機率低 ，將待12月利率會
議若通膨壓力持續偏高 ，預
估歐央將再升息一碼。
《歐元區PMI維持擴張但成長放緩，通膨壓力面臨
上行風險》
歐元區10月綜合PMI初值由9月的53.1降至51.9，
雖連續四個月高於 50、顯示經濟持續擴張 ，但成
長動能有所放緩 。法國受到大規模罷工與抗議活
動影響 ，商業活動下滑 ，成為主要拖累因素之一 ，
法國10月綜合PMI初值降至48.4，明顯落後其他
歐元區國家 。另一方面 ，能源及投入成本 上升迫
使企業提高售價，通膨壓力未明顯緩解。
中東緊張局勢延宕，布蘭特原油期貨價格升至116

美元/桶，TTF天然氣期貨價格升至96歐元/兆瓦時，
天然氣價格持續突破今年高點(統計至10/26)。
目前能源價格高於歐洲央行九月的基準與不利情
境預測 ，歐元區通膨面臨上行風險 ，不過未出現
明顯的二輪通膨效應 ，經濟成長則面臨下行壓力 ，
預期歐洲央行連續升息機率低 。此外，近期公債
殖利率上升亦發揮了緊縮效果 ，預估歐洲央行將
暫時按兵不動 。待12月份利率會議 ，屆時歐洲央
行將取得更新的經濟預測 ，可據此重新評估通膨
與成長前景 ，若能源價格及通膨壓力持續偏高 ，
不排除12月將再升息一碼 ，將存款機制利率調升
至2.75%。
針對近期主權債券殖利率大幅走升 ，預估歐洲央
行將密切關注其走勢 ，但長債利率的上升尚未達
到表明主權債券市場面臨顯著壓力的水平 ，應不
足以觸發政策調整。
資料來源：摩根士丹利，2026/10/22，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10。
能源價格高於歐央9月基準與不利情境預測，

重點摘要
6
• 政治與財政疑慮牽動十月歐
股走勢震盪 ，防禦與能源類
股漲勢掩蓋銀行股的拖累 ，
歐股收紅。
《政治與財政疑慮牽動十月歐股走勢震盪，防禦與
能源類股漲勢掩蓋銀行股的拖累，歐股收紅》
十月份以來，法國政治與財政擔憂升溫，加上中東
地緣局勢再度升級的擔憂推升能源價格，壓抑市場
風險偏好，牽動歐股走勢震盪。
全球債券市場波動加劇，法德利差急遽擴大導致銀
行股遭到拋售，市場擔憂主權債殖利率上升可能導
致銀行持有的主權債券部位產生損失，除此之外，
西班牙議會否決了首相桑切斯提出的住房法案，導
致該國宣布將於11月29日提前舉行大選，這給西班
牙銀行業增添了政治不確定性，風險趨避導致歐洲
銀行股跌勢較重。資金輪動至防禦型產業，食品飲

料與健護類股以逾9.7%漲幅領先，能源價格走高支
撐能源類股亦有逾7%的漲幅，支撐道瓊歐洲600指
數收紅0.9%。個別國家方面，法國CAC 40 指數下
跌1.5%，表現落後英國與德國股市 (統計至10/26)。
資料來源：Bloomberg，統計至2026/10/26，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10。

### Page 7
